FOREWORD
The material conteined in this draft manual hes been prepered
by the War Department in collaboration vith the Navy Department
in connection with the development of accounting procedures for
use of Financial Divisions of Civil Affairs Sections of U. S.
armed forces operating against Japan proper, except Formosa.
Parts of the manuel have been written in a style which implies
elements of finality, inflexibility, factuelity or specific
direction in respect to the subject matter. This has been
purposely done so as to meke the menual, or the eppliceble parts
thereof, more reedily uscble or adaptable by the senior commend-
ing officer as a source or medium of specific direction to his
subordinetes, Such implied or stated elements of finality or
specific direction in the manuel ere intended only as a sugges-
tion and a source of information insofer as the senior command-
ing officer is concerned, and are not in any way intended to be
mandatory.
The immediate purpose of this draft manual is to supply the
senior commending officer with suggested material regerding
finencial and accounting procedures, for his use in detailed
planning under finencial provisions of Civil Affairs directives
and the basic accounting guides supplementel thereto. . Thus the
contents of this draft manual pertain to the functions of Financial

=== Page 269 === (confidence 0.98)
TOREWORD (Continued)
Divisions of Civil Affairs Sections relative to such matters as
those outlined in the chart of "Organization for Financial Divi-
sion of Civil Affairs Section" which has been made a part of
this draft manual. Suggestions made in connection with such
functions concern the hendling of Civil Affeirs funds and sup-
plies, and necessery safeguerds relative thereto; the current
records for full accounting relationehips with Army and Navy
disbursing officers; the financial responsibilities of Civil
Affairs Sections with respect to national, governmental and
private enterprises; end the recording of data required in
connection with intergovernmentel settlements.

CONFIDENTIAL
RGANIZATION FOR FINANCIAL
DIVISION
OF CIVIL AFFAIRS
SECTION
FINANCIAL DIVISION
Within the scope of responsibility for civil affairs administration,
as delegated by higher authority, initiates or implements financial poli-
cies; controls foreign exchange and currencies; prescribes principles and
procedures relating to accounting, auditing and the disbursement and col-
lection of civil affairs funds; supervises and controls the disbursement
and collection of civil affairs funds, the keeping and auditing of related
accounting records, and the preparation of financial reports to higher
authority; and supervises or controls the financial policies and operations
of governmental, financial, commercial and industrial organizations to the
extent required.

CHAPTER I
CIVIL AFFIRS DISBURSING OFFICERS, GENT OFFICERS
AND REVENUE COLLECTORS
CIVIL FF.IRS DISBURSING OFFICERS
1. The Funds Control Branch makes recommendations to the
Director, Financial Division, regarding the appointment and quali-
fications of civil affairs diebursing officers and supervises their
training and activities. ny civil affairs officer, including a
Senior Civil affairs Officer, may be appointed as a disbursing
officer. The duties of a civil affcirs disbursing officer include
the receipt, custody and disbursement of civil affairs funds, the
recording thereof, and the reporting of such transactions to the
Accounts Brench. If the disbursing officer is assigned to or
operates under immedinte supervision of a regionel civil affeirs
office, the reports of his transactions may be forwarded to the
ccounts Branch through such regional office.
2. The disbursing. officer will be responsible for the safe
custody of all funds reccived by him and will be held personally
liable for ony loss which is due to negligence, or for misapplica-
tion or misuse of such funds. He also will be responsible for the
edministration and official acts of subordinates in his office and
for the proper use of funds intrusted by him to others as his agents.
He will take such precautions as ere necessary to insure thet only
properly authorized and supported disbursements ore made, and will
1 -1

=== Page 277 === (confidence 0.99)
maintain appropriete controls against the occurrence of fraud,
embozzlement or other misapplication of funds, whether by intent
or carelessness.
3. Other specific duties of a civil affairs disbursing of-
ficer are as follows:
a. ll official funds, accounts and documents will be
continuously segregated from personal funds and
other personel effects.
b. ll transactions will be currently recorded and each
day the fund balances will be verified.
c. Overages will be promptly recorded as receipts.
d. Shortages will be promptly reported to his
superior officer.
e. Funds on hend in exccss of the establishod maximum
balance or in excess of current requiroments will
be remitted as directed.
f. Such periodic reports as required will be
promptly propared and submitted.
All official funds, accounts, records and vouchers
will be made availablo for inspection or audit
when and as directed by higher authority.
h. ll accounting inquiries eddressed to his station
by higher euthority will be promptly answored,
furnishing the particulars or information
roquested.

1. ny apparent deficiencies in practice and proceduro
which mey come to his attontion, whereby revenues
are not being collected or funds are being wasted
due to extravagance should be promptly reported
to his superior officer.
AGENT OFFICERS
4. a civil affairs disbursing officer may appoint an agent
officer to assist him when it is impracticable for him to per-
sonally make certain payments but he is at all times responsible
for funds intrusted to the agent. Such appointment will be
effected by the issuance of a revolving fund werrant discussed
in Chapter VI. Appointment of en agent officer may be necessary
or desirable when the accountable disbursing officer is responsi-
ble for disbursing functions in an oxtensive nrea. The agont of-
ficer oither may be appointed to mako cortain spccific disburse-
monts or ho may be appointed for an indefinite period until
relieved. In the former casc, the cgont officer will make an
accounting to the accountable disbursing officer within 3 to 7
days, as the latter may dircct, after tho funds involved wore
advanced. In the latter casc, the ogont officer will make re-
ports to the disbursing officor monthly or more frequently, as
directed.
5. The agent officer has tho samo degrcc of responsibility as
does the accountable disbursing officer with rospect to the safe
custody of funds and porsonal liability for nogligent loss of
funds or for improper use thereof.

LCOTTEOTORS
6. Revenuo colloctors are civil affairs officers authorized
to receive or collect taxes, militery court fines, proceeds of
confiscated goods, end such other governmental revenues as may
be designated. Such officors will have no authority to make
disbursements from funds which they colloct but will pey oyer
the full amount of all collections to accountable:disbursing
officers. Revenue collectore are appointed and supervised by.
or under direction of the Chief, Governmental Agencies Branch.
7. It will be the responsibility of each revenue col-
lector to insure the collection of revenues over which he has
jurisdiction, to report and remit such collections in full, and
to safeguard all funds in his possession. With respect to
fuids received, tho revenue collector is porsonally liable in
the evont of negligent loss or improper use thereof.

RESPONSIBILITY

with responsibility for custody of funds and curroncics nd for
contral funding of civil affairs ectivitics and possibly military
activitios. Funds for conduct of those oporations normally will
be turned over to this branch by or through tho U. S. Treasury.
Department and/or the treasuries of other participating or
collaborating governments. ppropriate receipt will be furnished
by the Funds Control Branch to the governmental egencies concerned
in respect to all such funds.
CURRENCY AND POSTAGE ST.MP REQUIREMENTS

to the amount, categories ond denominations of authorized legal
tender required for uso within the occupied area. To the extent

facilitids within tho occupied area.

The Funds Control Branch will meke advances of currendies
or funds to civil affairs disbursing officers, military disbursing
officers; financial institutions, governmontal agenoies and com
mercial organizations on such basis and in such amounts as may be
prescribed end authorized. It will maintain a register of collections,
a check register and a cash disbursements register and will currently
report transactions to the Accounts Branch in a properly supported.
account current. Instructions for maintenance of these records and
preparation of this report are included in subsequent sections of
this manual.
7. Circumstances may exist whereunder it is desirable and feas-
ible to decentralize some part of the central funding functions to
strategically located banks. Any such bank will make advences within
the limits of the authority delegated to it and will maintain appropriate
accounting records comperable to those prescribed for the Funds Control
Branch.
8. If the bank is acting as an agent of the Funds Control Branch
it will submit appropriete current reports of its trensactions, and
the supporting data, for inclusion in its account current to the c-
counts Branch. Any bank, not acting as an agent of the Funds Control
Branch, will report its transactions direct to the ccounts Brench
on request.
FUNDING CIVIL AFFAIRS DISBURSING OFFICERS
9. Currency or fund advances to civil affairs disbursing officers
will be authorized and made under the direction of the Funds Control
Branch in such amounts as it may deem necessary. Each advance will
be supported by requisition from the disbursing officer in such detail
as may be required and will be evidenced by his signed receipt.
33

=== Page 283 === (confidence 0.99)
O MILITARY DISBURSING OFFICERS
10. Bunde advanced to a military disbursing officer may be
furnished against his official check, if such an arrangement has
been authorized, in which case no special accounting or reporting
problem will be involved for the Funds Control Branch. However,
this method of funding military disbursing officers generally
will not be followed since it would require the transmission of
the check by mail to the Treasury Department in Washington
with the consequent possibility of loss in transit.
il. A more likely procedure for funding military disbursing
officers will be one whereunder the disbursing officer will submit
his requisition for the required funds and give a. signed receipt,
rather than a check, for the amount advanced to him.
12. Insofar as U. S. militery disbursing officers are
concerned, this funding procedure will involve special reporting
of such transactions by the civil affairs administretion and by
the military disbursing officer concerned. The purpose of such
special reports is (a) to insure that the personal accountability
of the military disbursing officer is currently reflected in the
appropriate accounts of the U. S. government and (b) if a special
military currency is involved in the funding, to provide a
medium for effecting dollar backing of the advance, inasmuch as
the funds presumably will be used for pay of troops and other
activities covered by appropriations by Congrese.
13. Advances against the receipt of a U. S. Army account-
able disbursing officer will be independently reported, both
24

=== Page 284 === (confidence 0.98)
by the civil affairs administration and by the disbursing offioer
to the Fiscal Director, rmy Service Forces, War Department,
Washington 25, D. C. Such reports will be made currently by
radio as the transactions occur and will include (a) date of the
advance, (b) amount and currency involved, (c) name, and (d) dis-
bursing symbol number of the disbursing officer.
14. Such advances to U. S. Navy accountable disbursing officers
also will be independently reported by radio, and in the same
detail, to the Bureau of Supplies and Accounts, U. S. Navy De-
partment, Washington 25, D. C.
15. The War and Navy Departments, on receipt of such radio
report from the civil affairs administration and the confirming
radio report from the accountable disbursing officer involved,
will (a) record the dollar equivalent of the advance in their
departmental accounts as a purchase of currency by the disburs-
ing officer, and (b) report the detcils of the advance to the
U. S. Treasury Department for recording in its accounts, show-
ing date of advence; amount and currency involved; dollar equiv-
alent; name and disbursing symbol number of the accountable
disbursing officer; and the scrial numbers of the redio messages
in which the advance was reported to the department.
16. Similar independent special reporting requirements will
be observed in rospect to advances against the receipt of an
accountable militcry disbursing officer or peymaster of any other
perticipating or collaborating government. Such reports will
be rendored in such manner, in such detail and to such address as
the government concerned may prescribe.

CHAPTER III
CONTROL OF FUNDS
REQUISITIONS FOR CASH
1. When a civil affairs disbursing officer requires cash he
will prepare a requisition addressed to the Funds Control Branch, to
any bank which may be a currency custodien and authorized to honor
requisitions or, if authorized in emergency cases, to another civil.
affairs disbursing officer who may have on hand sufficient funds to
permit a currency transfer. The requisition will be prepared in an
original and five copies on a cash shipment form (Form No. 1 - Appendix
B) and will show the name and rank of the requisitioning officer, the
name and rank of the officer upon whom the requisition is made, and a
schedule of the denominations and amounts of currency and coin needed.
The requisition will be signed by the civil affairs disbursing officer
for whose account the funds are required and submitted by him in an
original and four copies.
. 2. The original, duplicate and triplicate of the requisition will

PHYSICAL CONTROL OF FUNDS
19. When currency or funds are transported from one point to
another, certain precautions are obviously necessary if loss end
theft are to be avoided. Currency end coin will be packed in
bags which will be sealed and labeled, and the bags will then be
packed in specie boxcs which will be sealed over all securing nails
and bolts. Insofar as is practiceble, the total valuo of the
money shipment should be equally distributed between the several
boxes.
20. The labels on the bags will show details of the contents
and the date, and will be signed by the remitting officer. t
stations where there are two or more officers, two officers will
sign the labels. To the extent practicable, coin of varying
denominations will be packed in separate bags and currency of
different denominations in separate packages. Specie boxes vill
be numbcred and sealed, and the number and contents will be shown
on a list to accompany the romittance.
21. The shipper will issue a cash shipment form (Form No. 1 -
Appendix B) in an original and four copics and disposition thereof
will be made in accordance with the instructions contained in para-
graphs 2 and 3 of this chapter. Upon arrival of the remittance,
tho rccoiving officor will inspect tho scals on tho boxos and bags
to verify that they are intact and will execute the "certificate of
custody" on the triplicato of tho cash shipment form and will

=== Page 293 === (confidence 0.98)
orm to the additional instructions set forth in peragraph"4
this chapter.
The contents of the bags will be jointly counted and
verified by the consignee and such other qualified porson as
may bo avnilable. In the event of cither a shortage or an
overage. the label on the bag will be ondorsed by both
persons, giving brief particulers of the discrepancy discov-
ered. In the case of a shortege, the particulars will be
telegraphod, whon such form of communication exists, to the
special civil affeirs disbursing officer in the Funds Control

in a covering revolving fund warrant.

.CHAPTER XIV
FUNCTIONS OF AUDI BRANCH
RESPONSIBILITY
1. The Audit Branch will have responsibility for the
examination, as to pronriety, •f the eccounts, records, vouchers
and official funds of civil affairs disbursing officers, agent
officers and revenue collectors and will direct the activities of
civilaffairs officers with respect to such examinations. In
addition, this branch will make such other eudits as may be directed
by higher authority. Accepted techniques and standards of operation
will be utilized.
AUDIT OF CIVIL AFFAIRS DISBURSING OFFICERS' ACCCUINTS
2. The accounts current of civil affairs disbursing officers,
together with the applicable schedules, vouchers and other sunnorting
data, wiln be transmitted to the Audit Branch by the Accounts Brench,
after comoletion of the posting, balancing and administretive examine-
tion functions in the latter branch. The objectives of the audit of
these accounts and vpuohers to be made by the. Audit Branch wilr be to
ascertain that the supporting data to record expenditures and col-
lections are complete and in order; that exnenditures have been
properly authorized; that all exnendituree and collections have been
properly recorded and accounted for; and that disbursing officers have
properly accounted for all funds for whion tney ere accountable.

data of the disbursing officer involved. After verifying and posting
the original of the journal voucher, the Accounts Branch will forward
the duplicate to the station from which the account current originated
together with an Accounting Inquiry form containing instructions as to

FILING

.CHAPTER XV
ACCOUNTING FOR CIVILIAN SUPPLIES
1. There has been prepared by the War Department e draft manual.
"Accounting for Civilian Supplies", which sets forth procedures per-
taining to the accounting for:
a. Supplies made available in confornity with military
necessity to the civilian population in areas liberated
or occupied by U. S. or Allied military forces, and
b. Goods and commoditics acquired by U. S. or Allied military
authorities, or egents thereof, for exnort from liberated
or occupied. areas.
2. The aforesaid manual does not incluie procedures pertaining to
initial procurement of sunplies dcstined for imnort into theaters of
operations for use therein as civilian supplies during the oriod of
military responsibility.
3. Relative to intergovernmental settlement for civilian supplies,
procedures set forth in the said manual provide for the current mainten-
ance of records for the use, as the case may be, of an eventual liquida-
tion commission, the Congress of the United States and Allied nations.
4. For the purposes of current and final settlement, it is a basic
policy of the United States Government that accounting for civilian supplie
be maintained in terms of money values as well as of quantities. In con-
formity with such policy the aforesaid manual provides accounting in terms
15 -1

values at theater headquarters level and in terms of quantities
a111evels.
5. The procedures provide that the meney value accounting records
for qivilian supnlies will be separate from the cash records which are
heroin set forth.

=== Page 349 === (confidence 0.98)
be legal tender. Three ledger accounts have beon established. The
first is a credit balance account which will show the amount of now
currency received. The second is a debit balance account which will
reflect the amount of currency unissued and in reserve available for
issuance. The third accqunt will carry a debit balance and will re-
flect the total amount of mutilated currency which has been destroyed.
The net of the three accounts will reflect the amount of military
currency in circulation at any given time.
b. Military Currency Received -- The amounts of new military
currency received will be credited to this account, end in each case,
the corresponding debit will be entered in the account "Militery
Currency Held in Reserve".
C. Military Currency Held in Reserve -- Upon receipt of new
military currency, this account will be debitcd and the offsetting
credit will be entered in the account "Military Currency Received".
As currency is withdrawn from reserve end deposited in bank accounts
or advanced to civil affairs and military disbursing officers, appro-
priete credits will be made to this account.
d. Mutilated Military Currency Destroyed -- Military currency
which has become mutilated or otherwise unfit for circulation, end
thereafter has been destroyed upon direction of competent authority.
will be accounted for by meens of a debit to this account and a credit
to the asset account "Mutilated Currency".

APPENDIX B ~ SAIPLE ACCOUNTING FORMS
INTRODUOTION
1. Included herein are sample accounting forms which have been
prepared for use in connection with the system of accounting and re-
porting outlined in this manual. The forms are adaptable for use in
any occupied country but certain of the forms should be altered to
the extent of also incorporating a foreign language translation, if
,applicable, so that where signatures of civilians are required or where
civilians are to receive the forms, the terms thereof will be readily
understood. For example, the text of receint voucher forms for use in
Japan should be printed in English and Japanese so that the receipt
will be intelligible to the holder.
2. As hereinafter indicated, many of the forms are to be prepared
in several copies. Except where the copies differ in content from the
original, a sample of anly the original has been shown. The forms should
be printed in matched sets and each form should be appropriately identi-
fied as "Original", "Duplicate", "Triplicate", etc. It is recommended
that wherever practicable the original and the several copies of forms
be printed on paper of differing colors to permit ready identification.
3. The. ccounting forms presented on the following pages were
designed to show the information considered essential or desirable but
were not intended to limit the size of the sheet, the spacing or number
of columns or the size and style of type to be us ed when the actual forms
are printed. These forms should be available in advance of operations
involving civil affairs administration and the factors just mentioned
should be taken into consideration when the forms are printed. If
B ~1

=== Page 355 === (confidence 0.99)
tont forms are not available, arrangements should be made locally
the printing of a temporary supply to provide for immediate requirements.

tion, together with an explanation of the number of copies to be prepared
and the prescribed disposition thereof:

=== Page 376 === (confidence 0.99)
APPENDIX C - DEFINITIONS AND TERMINOLOGY
TEAIRS OFFICER
A.civil. affairs officer is a member of the armed forces of
the United States or an Allied government who is assigned to duty with
the civil affairs section (or the military government section).
OIVIL AFFAIRS DISBURSING OFFICER
2. Those civil affairs officers who are assigned to disburse
civil affairs funds are designated as civil affairs disbursing officers.
Such officers may also receive and account for collections made by
themselves or by revenue collectors who report to them.
AGENT OFFICER
3. An agent officer is a civil affairs officer appointed to assist
a civil affairs disbursing officer by making disbursements when it is
impracticable for the disbursing officer to personally make such dis-
bursements. An agent officer receives money from the civil affairs
disbursing officer in the form of a revolving fund and renorts to the
latter with respect to the disposition of such fund.
REVENUE COLLECTOR
4. Revenue collectors are civil affairs officers who are appointed
to receive taxes, court fines, proceeds of confiscated or captured goods.
and other revenues which they ere authorized to collect. Revenue col-
lectors remit all collections to civil affairs disbursing officers and
have no authority to make distursements,
C - 1

=== Page 377 === (confidence 0.98)
HROUNTABLE OFFICER
5All civil affairs officers who are appointed as civil
re disbursing officers, agent officers or revenue collectors
are aocountable officers, being accountable for official funds.
ACCOUNTABLE BALANCE
6. Mach accountable officer's accountable balance comprises
all official funds for which he is responsible at any given time,
including amounts advanced to him, collections and other receints
which have come into his possession and amounts for which he has
not been relieved of accountability because of uneuthorized or un-
supported disbursements.
MAXIMUM BALANCE
7. The term "maxinum balance" has reference to the largest
amount of funds which a civil affairs disbursing officer is
authorized to have in his possession at any time and such amount
will be set forth in an official communication received from
higher authority.
C - 2

=== Page 378 === (confidence 0.99)
REVOLVING FUND
8. À cash fund turned over by a civil affairs disbursing
officer to an agent officor for use by the latter in making authe.
orized disbursements.
REVOLVING FUND WARRANT
9. The instrument whereby a revolving fund is officially created
and in which is stated the purposes for which the fund may be used.
COLLECTIONS
10. Amounts received by revcnue collectors and civil affairs
officers which represent revenues, e.g., court fines and proceeds
of confiscated or captured goods.
RECEIPTS
11. All funds received by accounteble officers are included
in the term "receipts", whether they be revenue collections, fund
transfers received or amounts collected as repayment of advances
previously made.
DISBURSEMENTS
12. Disbursem:nts are those payments by civil affairs dis-
bursing officers or agont officers from official funds, either in
cash or by check, including the payment of invoices, pay rolls,
operating expenses; the making of advances; and the transferring
of funds to other accountable officers.

=== Page 379 === (confidence 0.98)
Auvences are amounts disbursed which are subject to
ory at a later date.
14. Transfers are those payments of official funds by one
acgountable officer to another, including funds remitted by reve-
nue. collectors to civil affairs disbursing officers and funds
remitted by civil affairs disbursing officers to hoadquarters.
DEPOSITS
15. Deposits are funds received by a civil affairs dis-
bursing officer for later repayment to the original payor or
upon his order.
ACCOUNT CURRENT
16. An account current is the periodic statement required
of each civil affairs disbursing officer wherein he reports all
receipts and disbursements for the reporting period, and the
composition and disposition of his accountable balence at the
dlose of business on the last day of such period.
C - 4

=== Page 380 === (confidence 0.99)
ACCOUNTING INQUIRY
17. The term "accounting inquiry" refers to a form of communica-
tion addressed to a civil affairs disbursing officer by headquarters
for the purpose of eliciting an explanation in respect to any account-
ing transaction which requires clarification, additional documentary
support or justification.
CONTROL ACCOUNT
18. Control accounts are those accounts, contained in the general
ledger maintained by the Accounts Branch, in which are separately
recorded each major category of assets, liabilities, revenues and ex-
penses.
SUBSIDIARY ACCOUNT
19. A subsidiary account is one in which are recorded trans-
actions which relate to a certain tyoc of asset, liability, revenue
or expense, and the balance of which when added to the balances of
other related subsidiary accounts embraced within the samc major
category will equal the balance in the control account for such
major category. As an examplc, the control account, "Advances to
Accountable Officers", will reflect the aggregate amount owing
from all accounteble officers at a given date; in support of such
account there will be separate subsidiary account= to show the
amount owing from each accountable officer; anc the sum of the bal-
ances in the several subsidiary accounts must equal the balence in
the control account.
C - 5
